# Call sites a patched Instagram must have, checked across every dex of the APK by DexDiff.java.
#   single-call <method reference> in <caller method name>[|<caller method name> ...]
#   first-call <method reference> on <class>
#   first-call <method reference> on-type-named <GraphQL type>
#   first-call <method reference> outside <package prefix>
#   start-call <method reference> [in [static|instance] <(parameters)return, * for any run>] [calling [static|instance] <method reference>] holding <string> [<string> ...]
#   no-call <method reference> outside <package prefix>
#   next-call <method reference> after <method reference> [in [static|instance] <shape>] [calling [static|instance] <method reference>] holding <string> [<string> ...]
#   sole-call <method reference> replacing <method reference> [in [static|instance] <shape>] [calling [static|instance] <method reference>] holding <string> [<string> ...]
#   once-call <method reference> [in [static|instance] <shape>] [calling [static|instance] <method reference>] [sites <n>] holding <string> [<string> ...]
#   shared-call <method reference> [in [static|instance] <shape>] [calling [static|instance] <method reference>] [sites <n>] holding <string> [<string> ...]
#   story-loop-call <native method reference, its name may be *> [in [static|instance] <shape>] [calling [static|instance] <method reference>] holding <string> [<string> ...]
# A start-call, next-call, sole-call, once-call or shared-call rule picks its method by every string
# it names and its shape, and exactly one of Instagram's methods may answer that; name as much as the
# patch's own fingerprint finds the method by. A string with a space in it is written with \s for
# each space, and a backslash as \\. A string written as a field reference (Lclass;->name:type) is
# held by a method that reads or writes that field, for a method that loads no string of its own.
# For a method that holds neither, any of the five may say class-holding in place of holding: the
# strings are then its class's, held by its methods between them, and the method is the one of the
# shape in such a class. A * inside a class name stays in that name, so (L*;)V takes one object.
# Where a sibling has the same strings and shape, calling adds a call the method makes: it has the
# shape only when one of its invokes names a method reference matching the one written, with * as in
# a shape (and any run in the method's name). static counts an invoke-static alone, instance any
# other invoke, and neither word any invoke. A once-call or shared-call rule's method calls the hook
# once, or with sites <n> (2 or more) exactly n times, for a filter put on each of several reads in
# one method, or with sites returns once right before each return-object, however many it has. Where start-call and shared-call look for the hook in the other methods holding the
# strings, and once-call in every other method of Instagram's, they leave out a method another rule
# for the same hook picks, so two rules told apart only by calling each hold their own method, and a
# hook the patch puts in several methods gets a once-call for each.
#
# A token after holding or class-holding that is none of the above may be a literal, written const:0x
# and its value in hex, which a method holds by loading it; a call, written as its method reference;
# or a field or call on a Redex class written LX/*; for the class, with * for the member's name
# (LX/*;->*(LX/*;Z)V) or the name itself (LX/*;->A07:Ljava/util/List;). These are what stays the
# same from one 450 build to the next, since Redex renames the classes and most of the members
# between them. A single-call rule may name its caller more than once, joined by |, and a
# story-loop-call rule may write its native method's name as *.
## One guard per hot method. A guard the patch puts first in a method gets a start-call rule, which
# holds it first there and in no other method holding the same strings, and a once-call rule, which
# holds it to one call in that method and none anywhere else but the methods its other rules pick.
# start-call alone passes a guard written in twice, since the first of the two is still first.
#
# Taken from Hushfacebook's scripts/injected-mutation-contracts.txt
# (https://github.com/SysAdminDoc/Hushfacebook, commit 3a47363954eea853357e71e7cf3951a5ee984cee).
# GPL-3.0-only. Modified for HushGram (Instagram), 2026.
#
# The optional DM visual guard applies when its patch is explicitly selected. Both strings keep
# the shared voice endpoint separate. Its source remains excluded from default builds pending
# sender-side acceptance.
# Navigation binding fields below are pinned 450 verification data. Production discovery infers
# both concrete proxies, their shared tab owner and the factory's returned View before mutation.
# Each native setter calls remember once, before its framework listener binding. The returned
# factory View binds once, including the original systrace branch to its return.

start-call Lapp/hushgram/extension/instagram/settings/NavigationSettings;->remember(Landroid/view/View;Ljava/lang/Object;Landroid/view/View$OnLongClickListener;)Landroid/view/View$OnLongClickListener; in instance (Landroid/view/View$OnLongClickListener;)V holding LX/*;->*:Lcom/facebook/litho/LithoView;
shared-call Lapp/hushgram/extension/instagram/settings/NavigationSettings;->remember(Landroid/view/View;Ljava/lang/Object;Landroid/view/View$OnLongClickListener;)Landroid/view/View$OnLongClickListener; in instance (Landroid/view/View$OnLongClickListener;)V holding LX/*;->*:Landroid/view/View;

# The row built from views asks the same thing, and so does the first-run tip pointing at the bubble.
# The tip's collector holds no string of its own and sits in a coroutine emit Redex merges with other
# collectors: 453's held musicRowView from a merged neighbor, which 454 moved to another emit. On 454
# it's an emit on three builds (0F59.emit on 386014757, 0D6g.emit on 386014758, 0K9W.emit on
# 386014759) and a static coroutine step on 386014760 (0C9T.A0C), so the rule takes any arguments.
# It picks the tip by the fields it reads (the state's A02 and A05, the item's A00 and A01 and the
# answer A0H), by the object it builds behind a store fence and hands on through an emit, and by the
# coroutine step's label and result fields, which its merged neighbors share, and by the session
# lookup those neighbors make and it never does.

# Hidden chats (under Lock your messages) keep a hidden chat out of the inbox in three places: right
# before the inbox's list of chats is written to the holder the row factory reads it from, in the
# unread badge snapshot after it reads the thread store, and in that snapshot's folder count after
# it reads each folder tab's chats. On 452 the holder (06XM) writes that list (06XM.A0A) in two
# places on purpose, the subscriber to the inbox's state (0Ayk.A00, on 453 the lambda 0BHm.accept)
# and the inbox's own filter change (06XM.A05, on 453 04t1.A06), and each write gets the filter, so there's one rule per write. Each is handed
# the holder too, so a chat ticked or unticked in HushGram settings shows at once. Each rule picks
# its method by its shape, the holder's list fields and its kept flag, the store's filter it calls,
# and its call of the holder's starter (06XM.A01). On 454's 386014758 the holder is 01II, the
# subscriber the lambda 0AHX.accept and the filter change 01II.A08; the starter takes a String and a
# Function0 after the holder there, and the kept flag is A0J. The extension's redraw bridge, which the patch
# fills with the subscriber's own write of that list and call of that starter, has to leave the
# extension before it returns. No rule for the badge and folder hooks: the folder count holds no
# string of its own to anchor a rule on, and the snapshot's anchor is a shape, not a string.
# HiddenChatHookTest proves each hook and the bridge, and that each anchor is one method.

once-call Lapp/hushgram/extension/instagram/direct/HiddenChats;->inbox(Ljava/lang/Object;Ljava/util/List;)Ljava/util/List; in instance (LX/*;)V calling static LX/*;->A01(LX/*;Ljava/lang/String;Lkotlin/jvm/functions/Function0;)V holding LX/*;->A03:Ljava/util/List; LX/*;->A04:Ljava/util/List; LX/*;->A0J:Z LX/*;->*(Lcom/instagram/common/session/UserSession;Ljava/util/List;)Ljava/util/List;

once-call Lapp/hushgram/extension/instagram/feed/FeedFollowButton;->showSubscribe(I)Z holding null\scannot\sbe\scast\sto\snon-null\stype\sinstagram.features.feed.ui.rows.mediaheader.domain.uicontract.MediaHeaderUiState
# Hide activity bubbles (#475) hands ActivityBubbles.hide() each read of the two flags behind
# Instagram's own hide activity bubbles setting: in the per-item check of the class whose constructor
# reads that setting's rollout (07JI.A01 on 453), its has-the-setting read and its hide read, and the
# hide read in the feed's bubble builder (00i2.A03) and the Reels one (08zr.A01). 454's Redex inlined
# that constructor into the method making the setting (0HPM.A02 makes 08CB on 386014758), so the
# rollout's name left the class; its check (08CB.A01, 09OO.A01 on 386014757) is picked by the two
# Reels reposts grid modules it compares. Left out, the bubbles stay with the switch on; twice or
# elsewhere, it isn't the hook the patch wrote.
once-call Lapp/hushgram/extension/instagram/feed/ActivityBubbles;->hide(I)Z in static (Lcom/instagram/common/session/UserSession;L*;Ljava/lang/String;)Z sites 2 pooled holding clips_viewer_reposts_grid clips_viewer_self_reposts_grid
once-call Lapp/hushgram/extension/instagram/feed/ActivityBubbles;->hide(I)Z in (L*;Lcom/instagram/common/session/UserSession;L*;L*;IZZZZZ)L*; pooled holding feed_timeline_fan_club feed_contextual_chain
once-call Lapp/hushgram/extension/instagram/feed/ActivityBubbles;->hide(I)Z in (L*;L*;Lcom/instagram/common/session/UserSession;L*;L*;I)L*; pooled holding clips_viewer itas-android
# Chat history tools (#246) hands ChatPager.opened to the chat screen's onResume and ChatPager.closed
# to its onPause, the same pair of methods the chat lock hooks (the one pair logging the thread
# controller's DirectThreadController.onResume and .onPause). Not first in the method: both patches
# insert at the start and the chat lock, applied later, keeps that spot. Left out, the chat's name never
# gets its menu; twice or elsewhere, it isn't the hook the patch wrote.
once-call Lapp/hushgram/extension/instagram/direct/ChatPager;->opened(Ljava/lang/Object;)V in instance ()V holding DirectThreadController.onResume
once-call Lapp/hushgram/extension/instagram/direct/ChatPager;->closed(Ljava/lang/Object;)V in instance ()V holding DirectThreadController.onPause
# Read messages without the seen receipt hands MarkReadButton.opened and .closed to the same pair (#458),
# right after the chat lock's call, so the check mark reads the chat the lock just read. Left out, a held
# chat gets no check mark in its header; twice or elsewhere, it isn't the hook the patch wrote.
once-call Lapp/hushgram/extension/instagram/direct/MarkReadButton;->opened(Ljava/lang/Object;)V in instance ()V holding DirectThreadController.onResume
once-call Lapp/hushgram/extension/instagram/direct/MarkReadButton;->closed(Ljava/lang/Object;)V in instance ()V holding DirectThreadController.onPause
